1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: U/G Storm Hardening – Urd Dip – Gilchrest Road, Congers NY 
Project/Program Manager: Wayne Banker 
Project/Program Number (Level 1):  24792125 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 2023 
Estimated Date In Service: 2024 
A. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital: $1,900 
O&M:  
 
B.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
C. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
D. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
This is a Storm Hardening project to eliminate a double circuit distribution system along Gilchrest Road 
in Congers, NY to address poor service reliability on the distribution system associated with both small- 
and large-scale storms and equipment failure. This project will replace approximately 3,400 feet of 
overhead distribution with underground main-line distribution system between station breaker and rise 
on Rt 303 in Congers, NY (near pole# 60880/41429). The underground cable system will consist of three-
phase 750cu, CNJ 15kv cable, constructed with (4) four six-inch scheduled 40 steel reinforced conduit 
system concrete encased, with a spare duct system for a potential future circuit. This project will improve 
poor reliability issues associated with the overhead circuits during storm events. The underground 
system will provide an alternate path in the event of a contingency issue. The project design will reduce 
the exposure on a double circuit pole line configuration and provide an alternate path to reroute a 
portion of the circuit that is most prone to damage.  
 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
The scope of this project will be to storm harden the local distribution system and eliminate a double 
circuit configuration along the distribution path on Gilchrest Road in Congers NY. Currently the 
Congers circuits (22-3-13, 22-8-13) are fed from a double circuit pole line along Gilchrest Road. The 
purpose of this project will be to underground the 22-3-13 circuit between the Congers station breaker 
and rise on Route 303 (pole# 60780/41429).  Circuit 22-3-13 serves 492 customers, and circuit 22-8-13 
serves 573 customers; in total 1,065 commercial, residential, and industrial customers will benefit with 
this project. During past storms, this portion of Gilchrest Road has been damaged due to tree contacts 
resulting in large scale outages to those customers. Eliminating the double circuit will improve the 
reliability for customers in Congers, NY. 


--- Page 308 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (EIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 99 OF 132 
 
 
 
99 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
This project is in alignment with the Company’s goals to increase reliability through storm hardening, 
meet design standard, and upgrade aging infrastructure. In addition, the project addresses Corporate 
risk for Major Storms by providing storm hardening benefits. 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
The Company completed a detailed review to identify alternative routes. However, no options are 
available, and the underground distribution dip is the only solution.  This underground storm 
hardening project will benefit 3,400 customers, and will improve public safety.   
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
This is a Storm Hardening reliability project construction design, and as such a non-wires alternative 
(“NWA”) solution would not be suitable to address it. 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
The existing overhead system is constructed using double circuit design. During storm conditions, the 
probability exists that both circuits would be lost for any tree related outage or a motor vehicle 
accident (“MVA”) on any single pole. Relocating one of the circuits underground increases reliability 
and storm hardens the surrounding area. 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
The new underground mainline is required in this area to maintain system reliability and provide 
additional storm hardening benefits to the surrounding area. 
 
Summary of Financial Benefits and Costs (attach backup) 
1. Cost-benefit analysis (if required) 
 
2. Major financial benefits

Total current estimated cost for the above-mentioned project is $1.9 million. 
 
4. Basis for estimate 
 
Historical Company capital expenditures for similar underground distribution projects. 
 
5. Conclusion 
 
This project should be done to improve reliability and provide storm hardening benefits to customers 
in the Congers area.

Work Description: 
 
This is a Storm Hardening project to eliminate a double circuit distribution system along Thiells Mt. 
Ivy Road in Thiells, NY, to address poor service reliability on the distribution system associated with 
both small- and large-scale storms and equipment failure. This project will replace approximately 
10,000 feet of overhead distribution with underground main-line distribution system between Suffern 
Lane and the underground dip that crosses the Palisades Interstate Parkway (“P.I.P.”) (near pole# 
58623/44139 and pole# 589126/43319). The underground cable system will consist of three-phase 
750cu, CNJ 15kv cable, constructed with (2) two six-inch scheduled 40 steel reinforced conduit system 
concrete encased.  This project will improve poor system reliability associated with the overhead 
circuits during storm events. The underground system will provide an alternate path in the event of a 
contingency issue. The project design will reduce the exposure on a double circuit pole line 
configuration and provide an alternate path to reroute a portion of the circuit that is most prone to 
damage.  
 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
This project will storm harden the local distribution system and eliminate a double circuit 
configuration along the distribution path on Thiells Mt. Ivy Road in Thiells, NY. Currently, West 
Haverstraw circuits (27-6-13, 27-2-13) are fed from a double circuit pole line along Thiells MT. Ivy 
Road. This project will underground the 27-6-13 circuit between Suffern Lane and the P.I.P. crossing.  
Circuit 27-6-13 serves 1,092 customers, and circuit 27-2-13 serves 2,473 customers. During past storms, 
this portion of Thiells Mt Ivy Road has been damaged due to tree contacts resulting in large-scale 
outages to those customers. Eliminating the double circuit will improve the reliability for the 
customers in Thiells, NY.

Electric Engineering

1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: U/G Storm Hardening – Urd Dip – Route 202, West Haverstraw 
Project/Program Manager: Wayne Banker 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 23190661 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 2022 
Estimated Date In Service: 2024 
A. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital: $4,700  
O&M:  
 
B.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
C. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
D. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
This is a Storm Hardening project to eliminate a double circuit distribution system along Rt 202 to Rt 9W 
in West Haverstraw, NY to address poor service reliability on the distribution system associated with 
both small- and large-scale storms, motor vehicle accidents, and equipment failure. This project will 
replace approximately 6,000 feet of overhead distribution with underground main-line distribution 
system. The underground cable system will consist of three-phase 750cu, CNJ 15kv cable, constructed 
with (4) four six-inch scheduled 40 steel reinforced conduit system concrete encased. This project will 
improve public safety and will address poor reliability issues associated with the overhead circuits.  The 
underground system will provide an alternate path in the event of a contingency issue. The project 
design will reduce the exposure on a double circuit pole line configuration and provide an alternate path 
to reroute a portion of the circuit that is most prone to damage.  
 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
This project will storm harden the local distribution system and eliminate a double circuit 
configuration along the distribution path. The Village of Haverstraw is served with three 13.2kV 
distribution circuits; two of those circuits (Ckt. 27-4-13 & 27-8-13) have an auto-loop design that 
transfers the load automatically from one circuit to another in the event of an outage. This auto-loop is 
located between Westside Ave to Broadway to Samsondale Ave. The third circuit (27-3-13) feeds the 
most southern part of the Village of Haverstraw with limited distribution ties. As a result, this circuit 
(27-3-13) fails our contingency design criteria during peak summer periods.  Currently two of the 
distribution feeders are constructed as a double circuit (27-4-13 & 27-3-13) spacer construction pole line 
(approximately 6,000 feet in length) that starts at the West Haverstraw Substation and continues south 
on Westside Ave (a.k.a., Rt. 202) to the intersection of Route 9w and Rt. 202 in Haverstraw.  When an 


--- Page 316 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (EIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 107 OF 132 
 
 
 
107 
event occurs on this portion of the double circuit feeder either as a result of equipment failure, motor 
vehicle accident, lightning strike, etc., both circuits (27-4-13 and 27-3-13) are in jeopardy of being off-
loaded in order to expedite the repairs. Depending on time of year and loading of the two circuits our 
normal standard switching (cascading of load) will commence to isolate the fault and restore the 
majority of the customers within our design criteria. However, during peak summer loading periods, 
the alternate circuit (27-8-13) cannot support the entire load of two additional circuits (27-4-13 & 27-3-
13) due to cascading of load. As a result, circuit (27-3-13) with 2,800 customers may be without power 
until repairs are completed. This can be significant (6-8hrs) if a pole replacement is required. With the 
one circuit constructed to an underground design, the need to de-energize both circuits will not be 
required.  
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
The project is in alignment with the Company’s goals to increase reliability through storm hardening, 
meet design standard, and upgrade aging infrastructure. In addition, this project addresses Corporate 
risk for Major Storms by providing storm hardening benefits. 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
The Company completed a detailed review to identify alternative routes. However, no options are 
available, and the underground distribution dip is the only solution.  This underground storm 
hardening project will benefit 2,800 customers and improve public safety.   
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
This is a Storm Hardening reliability project construction design, and as such a non-wires alternative 
(“NWA”) solution would not be suitable to address it. 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
The existing overhead system is constructed using double circuit design. During storm conditions, the 
probability exists that both circuits would be lost for any tree related outage or a motor vehicle 
accident (“MVA”) on any single pole. Relocating one of the circuits underground increases reliability 
and storm hardens the surrounding area. 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
The new underground mainline is required in this area to maintain system reliability and provide 
additional storm hardening benefits to the surrounding area.  
 
Summary of Financial Benefits and Costs (attach backup) 
1. Cost-benefit analysis (if required) 
 
2. Major financial benefits 
 
3. Total cost 
 
Total current estimated cost for the above-mentioned project is $4.7 million.

PAGE 108 OF 132 
 
 
 
108 
4. Basis for estimate 
 
Historical Company capital expenditures of similar underground distribution projects. 
 
5. Conclusion 
 
This project should be done to improve reliability and provide storm hardening benefits to customers 
in the West Haverstraw area.

1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☐ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☐ Capital  ☒ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Underground (UG) Inspector/Technician 
 
Project/Program Manager: V. Anthony Rainone 
Jr. 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): N/A 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date:  Jan 2022 
Estimated Date In Service: N/A 
A. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital: $81.0 (annually starting in RY1) 
O&M: $9.0 (annually starting in RY1) 
 
B.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
C. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
D. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
The Company requires an UG Inspector/Technician to manage the day-to-day administration and 
coordination of capital and maintenance underground (“UG”) transmission and UG distribution 
construction projects, especially related to selective UG storm hardening projects. The inspector will also 
conduct field reviews of construction projects being performed by contractor labor forces to verify 
conformity with plans/specifications and that equipment and material supplied meets specifications. 
 
This new UG Inspector/Technician position will be responsible for assisting the UG project engineers 
in managing the field work related to the added workload associated with the storm hardening capital 
project initiative.  This is a long-term initiative which will require added resources to manage.  The 
inspector/technician would become a qualified electrical worker to provide site safety supervision 
within the Company’s substations and service territory.  The position would provide electrical and 
civil construction guidance in building a reliable UG cable system.  Employing construction 
management procedures, the position would be responsible for developing daily work reports and 
invoice verifications field reports; construction as builds’ coordination of local, municipal and state 
inspections; the completion of safety pre-job briefing with contractors; and managing the day-to-day 
construction and safety and environmental awareness activities related to these projects.  In addition, 
this position will perform residential, commercial, and industrial electrical inspections for new and 
existing electrical services throughout the Company’s service territory.  This position will also support 
the required commitment for storm response.  With the Inspector/Technician being electrically 
qualified, they will be available to perform field support during storm response as needed. 
 
The UG Inspector/Technician will also provide support in the solicitation and review of bid proposals, 
as well as coordinate and schedule the various operations of construction projects including equipment 


--- Page 320 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (EIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 111 OF 132 
 
 
 
111 
staging, material deliveries, and project/site safety. The position will prepare daily project progress 
reports, schedules, accident and damage reports, and switching orders, along with clearance requests, 
internal work application/permits, Contractor Oversight System (“COS”) reports, etc. as required or 
directed. The UG Inspector/Technician will advise the Project Manager and engineers on assigned 
project developments including schedules, costs, coordination efforts, potential areas which may cause 
project delay, and authorized expenditures to be exceeded. 
 
The annual cost for the UG Inspector/Technician is $90,000 per year and will be allocated 10% O&M and 
90% Capital. 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
Many of the new proposed projects that deal with new construction or the replacement of aged 
infrastructure in the utility sector are designed as underground systems which improves service 
reliability to customers both during normal operations and during storm events impacting the service 
territory.  This added capital work is a major driver in the request for additional support.  This UG 
Inspector/Technician is required to address the anticipated increased workload associated with the 
expected long-term Storm Hardening project, along with the added initiative to underground new 
construction.  This UG Inspector/Technician will provide the needed assistance to maintain schedule, 
verify quality control, and manage cost along with providing safety and environmental oversite to 
meet company and customers’ expectations.   
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
The addition of this position is in alignment with the Company’s goals to increase reliability through 
meeting design standard and upgrading aging infrastructure. In addition, the position addresses 
Corporate risk for Major Storms by providing storm hardening benefits. 
  
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
This work could be outsourced at a higher cost. Along with the additional cost, outsourcing this position 
would require a contractor to oversee other contractors, a situation the Company aims to avoid. 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
If the new position is not added, the increased workload could not be supported by the current UG 
engineers in the department. The work plan for the department will have to decrease in order to meet 
all project management requirements. 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
Additional UG projects could be completed resulting in additional benefits to customers such as storm 
hardening, increased service reliability, and additional load capacity.

1. Project / Program Summary

Project/Program Title: Sr. Service Layout Technician and Estimator 
 
Project/Program Manager: Ana Dunn 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): N/A 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date:  Jan 2022

O&M: $73.8 (annually starting in RY1) 
 
B.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000)

Work Description: 
 
The Company requires an additional Senior Service Layout Technician and Estimator to manage the 
increasing design needs of the Electric Distribution System. Responsibilities of this estimator will 
include: 
• 
Design major projects including system improvement/storm hardening/distributed 
automation along with design of ongoing maintenance programs; 
• 
Design interconnection for distributed generation projects, electric vehicle (“EV”) charging 
stations, and advanced metering infrastructure (“AMI”) make ready work orders; 
• 
Produce detailed point to point and layout estimates of capital and maintenance work for 
construction; 
• 
Review and approve design work by junior estimators; and 
• 
Train and assist junior estimators.  
 
The Senior Service Layout Technician and Estimator designs and reviews all point-to-point estimates 
of Capital, New Business, Distributed Generation projects, and ongoing maintenance programs. The 
design process includes field verification of existing assets, proposed equipment, and distribution 
engineering standards. The estimators produce a design package that includes work order point to 
point, layout and engineering write ups as needed. Work packages are delivered to the Operations 
Department for construction.  
 
Currently Line Technical Services (“LTS”) delivers an average of 2,850 design work packages annually. 
With continuing area growth, distributed automation and make ready work (AMI and EV) the 
Company will continue to experience design request growth in future years. 
 


--- Page 325 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (EIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 116 OF 132 
 
 
 
116 
The annual cost for the Senior estimator is $123,000 per year and will be allocated 60% O&M/40% 
Capital – clearance accounts. 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
A number of factors have led to an increased volume of design requests, as well as added complexity 
to project reviews.  This trend is only expected to accelerate going forward.  Some of these factors 
include: 
• 
Increasing numbers of Distributed Energy Resource projects;  
• 
Area growth represented with an average increase of New Business design work;  
• 
Responses to additional requirements resulting from New York State’s 2019 Climate 
Leadership and Community Protection Act (“CLCPA”), the related Accelerated Renewable 
Energy Growth and Community Benefit Act, and the New York Public Service Commission 
(“PSC”) Order on Transmission Planning Pursuant to the Accelerated Renewable Energy 
Growth and Community Benefit Act Commission Instituted New Case Proceeding; and 
• 
Additional requirements and anticipated workload related to the PSC July 2020 Order 
Establishing Electric Vehicle Infrastructure Make-Ready Program and Other Programs (“EV 
Make-Ready”) (projected 700 EV station projects for the O&R service territory).  
 
The design demands the additional response to State and PSC mandated program initiatives (CLCPA 
and EV Make-Ready) has driven the need for the addition of significant estimator hours that cannot be 
completed with the existing LTS Department complement.  
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
The addition of this position is in alignment with the Company’s goals to increase reliability through 
meeting design standard along with promoting the CLCPA’s goals. 
  
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
Require overtime while striving to meet internal and regulatory goals. Without this additional Senior 
Estimator, existing budgets will see an impact due to overtime costs and the risk of not meeting goals is 
high and/or quality of the work will be diminished. 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
The LTS Department is at risk of not meeting goals/deadlines (in service dates for customer service 
requests, including regulatory driven goals (e.g., CLCPA and EV Make-Ready)

Electric Engineering 
2024 
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☐ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☐ Capital  ☒ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: T&S Engineering Standards Engineer 
 
Project/Program Manager: Susmit Patel & James 
Heady 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): N/A 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date:  Jan 2024 
Estimated Date In Service: N/A 
A. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital:  
O&M: $103 (annually) 
 
B.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
C. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
D. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
The Company requests an additional Standards Engineer within the Transmission and Substation 
Engineering Department.   This Engineer will be responsible for developing, modifying, and updating 
all existing and future construction and material standards for the Transmission and Substation and 
Design Documentation Departments.  This Standards Engineer’s responsibilities shall include, but not 
be limited to: 
 
• 
Review of all current transmission and substation standards for conformance to exiting 
applicable code requirements; 
• 
Development of new standards to reflect new types of required installations and technologies; 
• 
Review of vendor and manufacturer capabilities and qualifications; 
• 
Consult with other utilities to include best management practices of others into the Company’s 
transmission and substation facility designs; 
• 
Review and approve standards prepared in conjunction with the Design Documentation 
Department; and 
• 
Assist Project Engineers with safety, code, material selection, vendor selection, and other 
appropriate matters.  
 
The Transmission and Substation Engineering Department is responsible for the engineering and 
design of all major capital and many major maintenance projects. In recent years, many codes and 
industry standards have been updated, modified, or superseded.  This new Standards Engineer would 
make corresponding updates to the Company’s transmission and substation standards library. 
Currently these updates are not formally documented in the standards library for the Company’s and 
developers’ future design considerations.  


--- Page 330 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (EIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 121 OF 132 
 
 
 
121 
 
The annual cost for a Standards Engineer is estimated to be $100,000, which would be allocated 100 
percent O&M. 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
There have been many technological and industry changes in recent years, and the Company’s 
Transmission and Substation Standards have not been able to be updated to reflect those changes. In 
addition, technological innovations have introduced new types of conductors, insulators, optical 
ground wires, circuit breakers, hardware, connectors, fittings, and more for consideration by utilities 
when constructing new or updating existing facilities.  Many of these innovations and developments 
require research into their applicability or advantages if they were to be incorporated into new designs.  
The manufacturers need to provide conclusive testing data and other utilities should be consulted if 
questions arise.  Familiarity with all these types of duties are the primary functions of a Standards 
Engineer.   
 
Having a current library of appropriate Transmission and Substation Construction Standards will 
reduce the time each Project Engineer has to spend researching manufacturer’s product and catalog 
changes, new product designs and applicability to the Orange and Rockland system. 
 
The Company has experienced an increasing number of inquiries from outside developers seeking to 
connect utility-scale solar generation and battery storage facilities to the Company’s transmission 
system.  Many of these connections require modifications of, or extensions to, the transmission system 
or the construction of new developer owned substations. All these facilities must be constructed to 
Company standards, and to date it has been a challenge to provide appropriate current construction 
and material standards to these developers. On many occasions the Company has had to rely on the 
use of AutoCAD drawings which are primarily selected based on similar prior projects.  Many times, 
numerous diverse project drawings introduce conflicts into the preliminary design and definitive 
expectations are not clearly communicated. Current applicable construction standards, that the 
proposed Standards Engineer would document, would significantly reduce this confusion and clearly 
communicate the Company’s expectations early in the project development process. This will help 
O&R support the achievement of the State’s goals as outlined in the 2019 Climate Leadership and 
Community Protection Act (“CLCPA”). 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
The addition of this position is in alignment with the Company’s goals to increase reliability through 
meeting design standard along with increasing hosting capacity and promoting the CLCPA’s goals. 
  
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
Continue to use outdated standards in conjunction with sample AutoCAD drawings and other relevant 
documents.

The estimate is based on market data and the company’s current benchmark rate without inflation. 
 
5. Conclusion 
 
This position should be added in order to update transmission and substation design standards, 
develop new standards for new types of installations and technologies, and improve the renewable 
interconnection process to support meeting the CLCPA’s goals.

1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☐ Capital  ☒ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☐ Operationally Required  ☒ Strategic 
Project/Program Title:  Electric Interruption Application Enhancements 
Project/Program Manager:  Brian Nugent 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): N/A 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date:  1/1/2021 
Estimated Date In Service:  12/31/2022 
A. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital:  
O&M:   $100.0 
 
B.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
C. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M:  0 
Capital: 
D. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
This request will be used to fund enhancements to the existing Electric Interruptions application.  The 
enhancements will increase the volume of Network Management System (“NMS”) events that can be 
automatically processed without engineer/analyst intervention.  In addition, other features and 
information validation will be built into the system to reduce errors.  The Company anticipates that the 
enhancements will be split between 2021 and 2022. 
 
 
Justification Summary: 
The current version of the Electric Interruptions application requires manual processing of thousands 
of NMS events.  The enhancements will allow these NMS events to be systematically identified and 
programmatically processed without the intervention of an engineer or analyst.  This will result in the 
saving hours of manual processing time each year.  In addition, the Company will explore 
opportunities for the automation of information population and form-based data validation.  This 
work will both reduce the number of man-hours needed to complete the processing of electric 
interruptions and reduce the opportunities for errors in the data. 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
The Company uses the Electric Interruptions application to process the electric outage information 
needed to calculate the System Average Interruption Frequency Index (“SAIFI”) and Customer 
Average Interruption Duration Index (“CAIDI”).  Both SAIFI and CAIDI are Company KPIs that have 
a negative revenue adjustment associated with them.  Errors in processing outages have the potential 
to lead to unnecessary financial disincentives and loss of Annual Team Incentive Plan (“ATIP”) goals. 
  


--- Page 335 ---

Risk 1 
If no action is taken, then the engineers/analyst will need to continue processing thousands of 
additional NMS events.  This practice is time consuming and increases the risk of errors. 
 
Non-Financial Benefits

Electric Engineering 
2023 
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☐ Project  ☒ Program  
Category:  ☐ Capital  ☒ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☐ Operationally Required  ☒ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Transmission System LiDAR Survey 
 
Project/Program Manager: J. Heady 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date:  3/15/2023 
Estimated Date In Service: 12/31/2024 
A. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital:  
O&M: $1,200 
 
B.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
C. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
D. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
This program involves complete Light Detection and Ranging (“LiDAR”) surveys of the lines designed 
as Bulk Electric System (“BES”) transmission facilities on the Orange and Rockland transmission 
system. The Company plans to utilize this survey information to develop as-built plan and profile 
drawings for the safe and reliable operation of these transmission facilities. 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
Many of the Company’s transmission facilities were constructed during the early electrification of the 
region.  Some facilities were constructed as early as the 1920s and 1930s.  The records the Company has 
related to these facilities are usually limited to lattice tower fabrication shop drawings and Plan and 
Profile drawings.  Construction records are typically not available, and the criteria used in the design 
of these lines is sometimes unknown.  Prior experience has shown that Plan and Profile drawings are 
typically a record of how a line was designed, not necessarily a record of how the line was built or 
currently exists.  An area of potential concern is the operation of these facilities such that appropriate 
phase conductor to ground clearance requirements are maintained.  As many of these facilities were 
constructed prior to the development of relevant codes and standards, the maintenance of appropriate 
clearances could be questioned.  Given the lack of as-built information, and the (unknown) heat 
stresses that could have been imposed upon the transmission conductors over time, a current survey of 
the BES transmission facilities is desired.   
 
An as-built LiDAR survey would enable these transmission facilities to be modeled in Power Line 
Systems - Computer Aided Design and Drafting (“PLS-CADD”) software to review the clearances 
under all operating temperatures and weather conditions. These PLS-CADD models could then be 


--- Page 339 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT____ (EIOP-1) 
 
 
PAGE 130 OF 132 
 
 
 
130 
used to determine if conductor temperature operating limits should be imposed upon any 
transmission facilities due to conductor sag limitations. 
 
The prioritization of the Company’s transmission facilities for LiDAR surveying and PLS-CADD 
modeling would generally be for the older BES facilities to be addressed first.  If desired, the non-BES 
transmission line facilities could be addressed later, based upon system voltage and anticipated 
operating conditions. 
 
The recommended priority for completing LiDAR surveys of the BES Transmission Facilities is: 
 
1. 138kV Lines 26/261 (Ramapo-Sterling Forest-Sugarloaf); 
2. 138kV Lines 27/271 (Shoemaker-Chester-Sugarloaf); 
3. 138kV Lines 53/530/531 (Lovett-West Haverstraw-New Hempstead-Burns); 
4. 138kV Lines 54/542/541 (Lovett-Stony Point -West Haverstraw-Burns); 
5. 138kV Lines 56/561/563/562 (Lovett-Bowline-Congers-Snake Hill-West Nyack); 
6. 138kV Lines 702/703 (Burns-Oak Street, Corporate Drive-Harings Corner); 
7. 138kV Line 51 (Ramapo-South Mahwah); 
8. 138kV Line 52 (Ramapo-Hillburn); 
9. 138kV Line 60/602/601 (Ramapo-Tallman-Monsey-Burns); 
10. 138kV Line 29 (Middletown Tap-Shoemaker); 
11. 138kV Line 28 (Sugarloaf 345kV Yard-Sugarloaf 138kV Yard); and 
12. 345kV Line 76 (Ramapo-Sugarloaf 345kV Yard). 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 The project will update our transmission line conductor field information which will allow for the 
analysis of our conductor clearances and support our facility ratings which all support our Loss of 
Transmission Corporate risk. 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
Conventional surveys of these Transmission facilities:  A conventional survey would be much more 
costly and time consuming.  In addition, it would not allow the transmission facilities to be modeled to 
determine if there are unknown operating limitations that need to be imposed upon these facilities for 
their safe and reliable operation. 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
Continued operation of facilities that may be in violation of applicable codes and standards while 
loaded within the limits of their Long-Term and Short-Term Emergency Ratings. 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
Increased safety and reliability of these transmission facilities. 
 
Summary of Financial Benefits and Costs (attach backup) 
1. Cost-benefit analysis (if required)

4. Basis for estimate

5. Conclusion 
 
This program should be undertaken to investigate, and improve upon if necessary, the safety and 
reliability of these transmission facilities under all operating conditions.

1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☐ Project  ☒ Program  
Category:  ☐ Capital  ☒ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: DSP Implementation Resource Requests: 1 Section Manager – Electrification 
Portfolio Management, 1 Project Specialist – Electrification Portfolio Management 
Project/Program Manager: Brian Picariello 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 21541705 
Status:  ☒ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: January 2022 
Estimated Date In Service: N/A 
A. Total Funding Request ($000)

☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000)

C. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M:

(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
The Company is requesting the addition of two full-time equivalents, a Section Manager, and a Project 
Specialist. Both positions will be dedicated to Electrification Portfolio Management (EPM) and will be a 
part of the Utility of the Future organization. 
 
The Section Manager and Project Specialist will focus on strategy and development of initiatives aimed 
at achieving green-house gas (“GHG”) reduction targets through beneficial electrification of the 
transportation and heating sectors, and the evaluation and implementation of non-pipes solutions 
(“NPS”).  
 
Activities will include: 
 
• 
Develop the Company’s electrification strategy in collaboration with internal and external 
stakeholders and as required by local, state, and federal regulations. 
o 
Analyze market model alternatives to facilitate informed decision making and assist in 
the formulation of the Company's strategic policy direction to leverage electrification 
to provide benefits to the Company and its customers. 
o 
Collect and analyze data on the adoption and usage of electrification technologies and 
leverage to inform and modify existing and future programs throughout the rate 
period, and to conduct targeted customer outreach. 
• 
Identify new business opportunities related to the growth of electrification solutions through 
new revenue models, NPS, demonstration projects, customer engagement tools, and a digital 
marketplace for energy products and services. 
o 
Engage internal business units, alternative energy providers, and customers to gain 
insight and identify barriers to adoption. 


--- Page 345 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT ____ (EIOP-2) 
 
 
PAGE 4 OF 32 
4 
 
• 
Lead the internal, cross-functional implementation of programs supporting the beneficial 
electrification of heating and transportation through a portfolio of energy solutions; activities 
include: 
o 
Develop programs to support customer engagement and encourage further 
electrification and increase customer awareness of the benefits of electrification; 
o 
Identify barriers to adoption of electrification technologies and partner with external 
stakeholders, such as developers and contractors, to address those barriers and 
encourage residential and commercial electrification throughout the service territory; 
o 
Conduct market research to maintain leading industry knowledge of current and 
projected customer behaviors; and 
o 
Integrate electrification technologies into the Company’s Distribution System Platform 
(“DSP”) plans to identify and leverage potential grid benefits which may be delivered. 
• 
Support Operations and Engineering to confirm the grid can absorb increased electrification 
loads, while maintaining system reliability, resiliency, and employee and public safety. 
o 
Minimize grid impact of electrification solution through modeling and analysis of the 
distribution system. 
o 
Leverage installed capacity and optimize utilization as much as possible to serve new 
load from electrification technologies. 
o 
Design new and efficient internal processes to execute programs (e.g., a framework for 
non-pipe-solutions). 
• 
Support coordination efforts across utilities impacted by the same state-wide initiatives: 
o 
Support response to regulatory proceedings; and  
o 
Organize requests (e.g., non-pipe solutions). 
• 
Represent the Company at New York Public Service Commission (“NYPSC”) and industry 
conferences, working group meetings, and other external stakeholder efforts to promote and 
reduce barriers related to the adoption of electrification solutions. 
• 
Manage consultants and third-party partner relationships to ensure successful outcomes. 
 
These activities will be organized, prioritized, and shared between the two positions at the discretion 
of the Section Manager, with guidance from the Utility of the Future team and its leaders.  
 
The Company is requesting both positions, the Section Manager and the Project Specialist, begin in 
January 2021. The annual cost to the Company in Year 1 is $140K for the Section Manager and $120K 
for the Project Specialist, respectively. 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
Electrification is critical to the future of the Company, its customers, and its external stakeholders. The 
immature state of the electrification industry requires additional focus and support from the Company 
to explore, increase adoption, and develop new business models with respect to electrification.  
 
Electrification technologies are in their early stages of adoption, when customer awareness and 
understanding are low, and barriers to adoption are significant. Many customers who have adopted 
electrification technologies to date have been first movers who sought out information proactively or 
acted from a desire to reduce their carbon footprint. To encourage adoption by the critical mass of 
customers required to make the technologies (and the Company) successful, the Company must 
increase and improve its customer engagement. The Company must develop a sustainable strategy for 
encouraging the adoption of electrification technologies through innovative business models and 
addressing barriers to adoption. The Company must the use that strategy to execute demonstrations 
and pilots, develop incentives, and ultimately communicate the benefits of these technologies to 


--- Page 346 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT ____ (EIOP-2) 
 
 
PAGE 5 OF 32 
5 
 
customers in an organized and effective manner. A dedicated program is needed, and the positions 
requested will support its successful execution.  
 
Similarly, the models for ownership of these technologies is unclear and subject to evolve as adoption 
increases. Currently, customers are typically implementing a single technology in isolation (e.g., 
purchasing an electric vehicle OR installing a rooftop solar panel). As electrification adoption increases 
and customers begin to implement multiple technologies in a portfolio of solutions (e.g., installing a 
battery AND going on a TOU rate), a layer of complexity will be added to electrification programs. The 
Section Manager and Project Specialist will help manage this complexity and adjust as needed based 
upon real-time findings.  
 
The increase in state legislation and related regulatory proceedings, including the following, will 
increase the workload volume supporting electrification. 
 
• 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (“CLCPA”); 
• 
Focus on EVSE&I Programs by the New York Department of Public Service; 
• 
NYPSC Order Authorizing Utility Energy Efficiency and Building Electrification Portfolios; 
and 
• 
The NYPSC’s Proceeding on Motion of the Commission in regard to Gas Planning Procedures 
which involves exploration of demand-side measures through NPS to address system needs, 
and support the State’s decarbonization goals.  
 
Electrification is a positive opportunity for all stakeholders involved and will remain a focus of the 
Company for years to come. The Company must support the opportunity with a dedicated team to 
manage the iterative and hands-on process these early stages require. Maintaining the workload 
associated with these needs will require two full time employees: a Section Manager and a Project 
Specialist.  
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
These positions will help the Company achieve its Clean Energy Future as detailed across a variety of 
plans, initiatives, and regulatory requirements. Specifically, the CLCPA has established ambitious 
clean energy policies and the Company has oriented toward these goals. The dedicated EPM team will 
support carbons emissions reductions across the integral transportation and heating sectors.   
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
 
Alternatively, the Company could hire a contractor to perform this work. This option is not 
recommended as encouraging and managing beneficial electrification is critical to the Company’s long-
term sustainability, is a core competency the Company aims to develop, and will remain an integral 
part of the Company’s operations in pursuit of a Clean Energy Future. In addition, hiring a contractor 
would come at a cost premium for the Company. Launching a dedicated EPM team will set the 
Company up for future success and minimize costs to customers.  
 
 


--- Page 347 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT ____ (EIOP-2) 
 
 
PAGE 6 OF 32 
6 
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
 
The Company could instead perform this with only one position. This leads to the same outcome as 
described below in the “Risk of No Action” section below.  
 
 
Risk of No Action 
Give the consequences, including enterprise risks that might arise by not doing the project/ program. Quantify 
the risks, if applicable. 
 
Risk 1 
 
Not performing this work would undermine the adoption of electrification throughout the Company’s 
service territory, thereby frustrating the achievement of state-wide decarbonization goals. However, 
this work must be completed, and, if no additional positions are hired, then the workload will be 
picked up by the existing Utility of the Future organization. The Utility of the Future organization’s 
workload volume is at a maximum, and other programs supporting the CLCPA goals would suffer.  
 
Risk 2 
 
If the Company abandons this program it risks customers remaining unaware of the impacts 
electrification technologies have on the grid. This could lead to customers installing systems without 
proper communication to the Company and could decrease system reliability due to capacity issues.  
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
There are several non-financial benefits to implementing an Electrification Portfolio Management 
Team.  
 
• 
Improved awareness of the increased importance the NYPSC, NY State, and other external 
stakeholders have placed on electrification as a key component to achieving decarbonization 
goals.  
• 
Improved communication about electrification among the organization and community.  
• 
Clearer accountabilities in dividing responsibilities among the Utility of the Future Team.  
• 
Increased focus on electrification driven by this team will improve the likelihood of achieving 
the state’s decarbonization goals.

Electrification will be critical to the Company’s pursuit of the Clean Energy Future. Having a dedicated

increased adoption and an increased rollout of the derivative benefits provided by electrification. This 
must be done to achieve success for the Company.

1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☒ Project  ☐ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Monsey NWA 
Project/Program Manager: MD Sakib 
Project/Program Number (Level 1):  
23837959-0008 
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☒ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 2021 
Estimated Date In Service: 2022 
C. Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital: $27,237 
O&M:  
 
D.  
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M:

E. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
F. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description: 
 
As part of the 2018 Electric Rate Case, the Company received approval to recover the program costs for 
a Non-Wire Alternative (NWA) solution in the Monsey area. The program costs were estimated to be 
$16M to be amortized over ten years. The Company has redesigned this NWA solution and prepared a 
new supporting benefit-cost-analysis (BCA). The Company has reviewed this new approach and 
positive BCA with DPS Staff per the NWA process. DPS Staff is supportive of the redesigned NWA 
solution in the Monsey area.  The expected costs of the new program are outlined below.  
  
The Company has selected a vendor to install, operate, maintain, and own a battery energy solution to 
address peak load demand and defer capital investments in the Monsey area. The Company will have 
exclusive rights of dispatch to the battery to address the distribution system’s peak. The Company has 
identified the following key milestones associated with this project:  
 
• 
2022: three batteries installed for Monsey (only activity completed in Rate Year 1); 
• 
2028: Burns substation reinforcement completed; and 
• 
2032: New Viola substation constructed. 
 
The Company will incur the following costs estimated at approximately $43 million over the course of 
this project. The estimate includes program and (internal) portfolio administration costs, 
interconnection costs, the cost of charging, and the cost of traditional capital investment portion of this 
project.  
 
 
 


--- Page 351 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT ____ (EIOP-2) 
 
 
PAGE 10 OF 32 
10 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
In Q4 2017, the Company issued an RFP for a NWA solution to defer traditional distribution system 
capital investments in the Monsey area. The Company selected a battery energy storage solution from 
EOn/RWE to reduce peak electric load within the area served by the Monsey substation and banks. 
The NWA also reduced peak electric load on three Monsey distribution circuits and associated 
distribution circuit ties.  
 
After failing to find adequate sites to locate the battery storage systems, the Company revised the 
scope of work and identified an alternative solution to achieving the needed load reduction. 
 
The new solution incorporates the NWA solution with a smaller traditional capital investment to meet 
the deferral need. Specifically, the new solution proposes three battery storage systems be installed in 
the Monsey area to reduce peak load demand coupled with a transformer installation in the Burns 
substation. Combined, these efforts defer construction of the new Viola Road Substation project from 
2022 to 2032.  
 
The additional funds requested are required for the operational and financial reasons outlined below: 
 
• 
The new solution defers the construction of a new substation by providing load reduction 
during system peaks in the impacted area; 
• 
The new solution passes the benefit-cost analysis parameters required for any NWA solution; 
• 
As compared to the original NWA solution, the new solution defers a larger capital 
investment, represents a larger net present benefit value for the Company, and results in a 
higher benefit-cost ratio; and  
• 
The new solution reduces risk by transferring ownership of the battery to the vendor.  
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
• 
The Monsey NWA Project enhances customer experience by deferring capital investments 
through leveraging energy storage to reduce peak load demand. 
• 
The Monsey NWA Project achieves operational excellence by advancing system design to 
integrate distributed energy resources and help achieve the Climate Leadership and 
Community Protection Act’s (CLCPA) goals of the state. 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
 
The Company could continue to try to site the original Monsey NWA solution. However, the project 
would likely be denied by the Town Planning board. No alternative sites were identified during an 
exhaustive search and it is unlikely the Company would be able to move forward with the original 
NWA solution.  
 
This alternative is not advised because it could antagonize Town Planning Board officials and risk 
undermining the Company’s relationship with local officials. In addition, this option would also risk 
negatively impacting public opinion of NWAs due to negative feedback for the Monsey project in 
general.  


--- Page 352 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT ____ (EIOP-2) 
 
 
PAGE 11 OF 32 
11 
 
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
 
The Company could pursue a new NWA solicitation to identify other alternatives to meet the need for 
peak load reduction in the Monsey area. However, this would delay implementation of the NWA 
project and have the effect of lessening project benefits. Given the focus the New York Public Service 
Commission has put on NWAs, and the prospect they provide as examples for future innovative 
projects, further delay is not advised.  
 
Risk of No Action 
 
If no action is taken, the Company would be forced to implement the traditional solution being 
deferred. This results in the following risks to the Company and its customers: 
 
Risk 1 
 
Future NWA projects could suffer from (i) the lost experience in executing this NWA project and (ii) a 
declining interest among customers. 
 
Risk 2 
 
The net benefits of the NWA solution would not be realized for the Company and its customers.  
 
Risk 3 
 
The Company would not realize 15MWs of energy storage toward meeting its State goal of 3,000 MW 
of energy storage by 2030. 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
• 
Learning opportunities for the Company concerning the integration of energy storage systems 
within its distribution infrastructure (e.g., permitting, siting)  
 
Summary of Financial Benefits and Costs (attach backup) 
1. Cost-benefit analysis (if required) 
 
N/A 
 
2. Major financial benefits 
 
N/A 
 
3. Total cost 
 
The total current estimated cost for the above-mentioned project is $42,839,671. 
 
4. Basis for estimate 
 
The estimates are based on costs provided from the vendor to provide the services detailed. Other 
components of the estimates are based on BCA Handbook guidance.

Project/Program Title: Pomona Site Maintenance

Work Description: 
 
This program seeks to fund the Operation & Maintenance (“O&M”) costs required for the battery 
energy storage system (“BESS”) within the Pomona Distributed Energy Resource (“DER”) Program.  
 
In 2015, the New York Public Service Commission (“Commission”) approved the Pomona DER 
Program its rate order in Case 14-E-0493.1 
 
The Pomona DER Program’s objective is to defer the construction of the Pomona Substation and 
associated facilities by implementing a portfolio of energy storage and demand side management 
(“DSM”) programs that provide peak load reduction.  
 
As part of this program, the Company will manage a BESS installed at 23 Diltz Road in Pomona, 
adjacent to the Company’s Ladentown switching station. The Company has partnered with Key 
Capture Energy (“KCE”) to plan, design, install, operate, and maintain this utility-scale battery, sized 
at 3MW and 12MWh.  
 
The battery has been energized as of December 22, 2020, and will be operational in Q1, 2021. Following 
this, the battery will begin normal operations and the Company will begin incurring O&M costs.  
 
 
 
1 Case 14-E-0493, Proceeding on Motion of the Commission as to the Rates, Charges, Rules and Regulations of Orange and Rockland Utilities, 
Inc. for Electric Service, Order Adopting Terms of Joint Proposal and Establishing Electric Rate Plan (issued October 16, 2015) (p. 18) 
(“2015 Rate Order”). 


--- Page 356 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT ____ (EIOP-2) 
 
 
PAGE 15 OF 32 
15 
 
As set forth in the 2015 Rate Order, O&M costs were not included as part of the Pomona DER Program. 
The Company is requesting funding detailed below to cover annual site maintenance for the BESS. 
There are three categories of O&M expenses: 
 
Annual Battery Operation & Maintenance: upon the in service date of the energy system, KCE will 
manage the operation and maintenance of the energy system. This includes all operational, 
engineering, maintenance, repair and such other technical services as may be necessary to the 
operation and maintenance of the system.  
 
Water Infrastructure Maintenance: as part of the site plan and building permit approval, the 
Company was required to install a dedicated fire hydrant on the site for safety purposes. The 
dedicated fire hydrant required a new, dedicated water line which requires maintenance. Maintenance 
on the water infrastructure will be provided by a qualified contractor.  
 
The objective of all activities is to maintain appropriate and constant water pressure and water flow 
throughout the system.  
 
Communications Network Fees: To increase cybersecurity the Company will establish a dedicated 
virtual private network (“VPN”).  VPNs improve an asset’s cybersecurity by encryption on a non-
externally routable network to establish a point-to-point connection between end users. The Company 
plans to engage Verizon to maintain this dedicated VPN and is requesting the annual fees associated 
with providing this service.  
 
After installation of the battery, the Company will close out the Pomona DER program and file for the 
incentives approved in the 2015 Rate Order. The agreed-upon incentives are as follows: 
 
• 
Cost Savings (up to 50 basis points): 1.0 basis point for each 1% reduction in cost per MW 
compared to the cost of the proposed Pomona Substation; and 
• 
Amount of Load Reduction Achieved (up to 50 basis points): 1.0 basis point for each 0.1 MW of 
load reduction achieved above 3.0 MW. 
 
 The Pomona Project will achieve 4.1 MW of load reduction. Based on the approved incentive 
calculation the Company earned 11 basis points under the Amount of Load Reduction Achieved 
incentive. This equates to $30,140, which will be recovered through the ECA surcharge.   
 
Justification Summary: 
 
O&M expenses requested in this whitepaper are needed to continue successful operation of the 
Pomona DER Program, specifically the newly installed battery energy storage system identified in the 
“Work Description” above. All O&M expenses support the safe, secure, and reliable delivery of energy 
– via dispatch of stored energy – to the Company’s customers.  
 
Justification for each expense category is as follows: 
 
Annual Battery O&M: Annual battery O&M will mitigate the degradation of the battery and enable 
the Company to maintain required system performance levels to deliver energy reliably throughout 
the life of the system. KCE installed the battery and is best suited to perform these maintenance 
activities. These activities are the baseline required to operate the BESS. 
 
Water Infrastructure Maintenance: this will prevent degradation and maintain the appropriate water 
pressure and water flow for safe operations as required by the Ramapo Fire Department.

PAGE 16 OF 32 
16 
 
Communications Network Fees: this will minimize the risk of system network vulnerabilities and 
malicious cyber-attacks and enable the Company to deliver energy securely. 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
• 
Addressing the required water infrastructure maintenance and communications network fees 
improves the safety and security of operations, one of the Company’s primary goals. 
• 
The Pomona DER Program enhances customer experience by deferring capital investments 
through leveraging energy storage to reduce peak load demand. 
• 
The Pomona DER Program as a whole and, specifically, the BESS achieves operational

Leadership and Community Protection Act’s (“CLCPA”) goals. 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
 
The Company could perform these O&M activities internally, instead of soliciting support from a third 
party. The expertise required to perform these activities will be developed over time but are not in 
place currently. Therefore, performing the activities internally would require rapid development of a 
new internal capability. The acquisition cost of this capability – either through training or a new hire – 
would be high. The work is also still very specialized and is not certain to be a continued need for the 
Company. Finally, the work is intermittent and would not support the full time of whoever is assigned 
to manage this work.  
 
Alternative 2 description and reason for rejection 
 
The Company could choose a different vendor based on cost. This would require the re-solicitation of 
the RFP and would ultimately lead to a delay in this project. Finally, selecting another vendor could 
lead to inefficiency driven by required coordinated between the original equipment manufacturer and 
the newly selected O&M vendor.  
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
 
If the annual battery O&M is not performed, the asset will degrade, and its lifespan will shorten. 
Degraded assets pose a safety risk to the Company and its customers due to the unpredictable nature 
of their operations. There will be increased risk of system level performance dropping below required 
levels. There will be increased risk of energy dispatches failing. Not performing O&M on this asset will 
negate its benefits and run counter to the Company’s overall goals. 
 
Risk 2 
 
If the water infrastructure maintenance is not performed, there is increased risk of fire hydrant failure. 
In the event of an emergency, this directly impacts the safety of customers around the site and first 


--- Page 358 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT ____ (EIOP-2) 
 
 
PAGE 17 OF 32 
17 
 
responders and fire departments. There is also risk of developing water stagnation, a hazardous 
environment that leads to water pollution and impacts the community.   
 
Risk 3 
 
If the communications network fees are not approved and the associated activities are not performed, 
there is an increased risk of a malicious cyber-attack. The attack could take various forms, including 
the improper dispatch of energy which would jeopardize the safety and reliability of the Company’s 
energy system. With control of the asset, cyber attackers could hold it inoperable or threaten to take 
action until the Company responds to their requests.  
 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
In general, the O&M activities outlined in this request support the successful implementation of the 
Pomona DER Program which supports the State and Company clean energy goals. These activities also 
explicitly increase safety, reliability, and security for the Pomona DER Program and its impacted 
customers. Finally, this program represents a learning opportunity for the Company should the 
capability move internally in the future.

This program should be implemented to support the successful execution of the overall Pomona DER

Work Description: 
 
The objective of the Customer Enablement Initiative is to increase the adoption of emerging clean 
energy technologies through customer enrollment and participation in a variety of potential programs.  
The Company seeks to provide a customer experience that supports a sustainable and clean energy 
future.  Through this initiative, the Company will use technology and innovation to be pro-active in 
providing customers with tools and information that they need to participate in a clean energy future.   
The Company is requesting funding to execute these programs, including coverage for any third-party 
program administration fees, funding for rebates and incentives, event space and equipment rentals, 
developer fees for online services, and paid media marketing support. The Company is also requesting 
a dedicated FTE, i.e., a Project Specialist, in order to manage and implement these programs. The 
Project Specialist will be a member of the Utility of the Future organization.     
 
To date, the Company and, specifically, the Utility of the Future organization, has focused on the 
upfront strategic, regulatory policy, and planning work required to understand the Company’s role in 
shaping the clean energy future through emerging clean energy technologies. Currently, heat pumps, 
electric vehicles (“EVs”), and energy storage systems are the emerging clean energy technologies the 
Company views as having the most significant impact on supporting the State’s clean energy goals. 
Now, it is important the Company move into program implementation and launch programs that 
increase customer awareness, engagement, and adoption. These programs will provide customers with 
the tools and information needed to make informed decisions when contemplating the adoption of 
these emerging clean energy technologies. With knowledge of a suite of distributed energy resources 
and available incentives, the Project Specialist can facilitate customers understanding of how these 
technologies interrelate, and can be adopted to benefit customers, and support the State’s electrification 
goals. 
 


--- Page 362 ---
 
 
EXHIBIT ____ (EIOP-2) 
 
 
PAGE 21 OF 32 
21 
 
The Project Specialist will be responsible for implementing programs like the exemplary list outlined 
below. Due to the rapid change of customer needs, emerging technologies, and developing markets, 
the Company requests flexibility in program funding to allow for adjustments throughout 
implementation. 
 
Customer engagement efforts will include the development of outreach and education materials and 
events, incentives, and tools which will increase the adoption of emerging clean energy technologies to 
further the Company’s progress toward meeting the Climate Leadership and Community Protection 
Act’s (“CLCPA”) goals. In addition to overseeing and implementing Customer Enablement programs, 
the position will be responsible for the following: 
 
• 
Manage internal and external efforts to communicate the benefits and encourage the adoption 
of electrification technologies, including the support of material development for outreach and 
education 
• 
Deploy tools (e.g., total cost of ownership calculators) to assist customers in understanding the 
benefits of electrification solutions, and empower them to make informed decisions     
• 
 Oversee the development of marketing materials and media relations strategies to 
communicate goals and benefits of REV and CLCPA to customers and other stakeholders 
• 
 Collect and support data analysis to glean business insights and inform all other Customer 
Enablement Initiative implementation 
o 
Examples include analysis of energy-use patterns to inform outreach strategy; analysis 
of website traffic to improve user-interfaces; analysis of customer feedback (via 
surveys) to adjust program design 
o 
The Data Analytics Program, as requested in the Customer Operations Panel, will 
amplify the success of the Project Specialist through increased access to data and/or 
data analytics software 
o 
All these efforts could inform targeted marketing by focusing on customers, 
developers, and clean energy technology provides with the greatest adoption potential 
• 
Track program progress through engagement metrics, communicate program progress across 
the UotF organization and Company 
• 
Manage relationships with customers and third party vendors; execute or confirm associated 
rebates, incentives, or payments for services across program milestones 
• 
Represent the Company at NYPSC and industry conferences, working group meetings, and 
other external stakeholder efforts in an effort to be able to effectively promote the adoption of 
electrification and clean energy solutions 
• 
Manage consultants and third-party partner relationships

Justification Summary: 
 
In order to achieve the objectives of the clean energy future, the Company must engage its customers 
as partners and support them to make informed decisions regarding the adoption of emerging clean 
energy technologies. Ultimately, it is the Company’s customers that must adopt technologies that 
support the achievement of the CLCPA’s goals. The goal of this program is to enhance customers 
understanding and selection of the choices available to them. To do this, the Company must engage 
with customers in new ways, increasingly digitally or through mobile applications, to facilitate an 
environment where the customer has choice, control, and convenience.  
 
To establish choice, customers must be well-informed regarding the benefits of these new technologies 
and the optimal way to deploy them to maximize their benefits (including for their communities and 
society). Therefore, the Company plans to launch programs that focus on increasing customer 
engagement through activities like rebates, surveys, educational events, as well as outreach materials.  
 
These are new technologies with relatively low awareness. Therefore, to be successful, the Company 
requests an additional FTE and funds to develop and support the programs and tools discussed above. 
The Company anticipates rate year one to focus on planning and rate years two and three to focus on 
implementation. As a result, the budget in rate years two and three are higher than rate year one. Still, 
the Company expects to launch these new tools as early as possible to maximize their impact during 
the Company’s next rate plan, and requests flexibility across the three years to allow for changes as the 
Company learns more about customer behavior and their response to initial program offerings.  
 
The Company also plans to develop internal and external-facing tools to support decision-making. 
Providing customers with tools to make informed decisions and leveraging customer data through a 
data analytics platform can revolutionize the way the Company interacts with its customers.  
 
Similarly, better understanding customer behavior can help minimize the impact on the traditional 
distribution system that a mass deployment of electrification technologies would cause. If the 
Company can predict actions based on a customer profile, the Company – in collaboration with the 
system planning team – can design programs that incentivize select customers and avoid the need to 
increase installed capacity.  
 
The customer can exercise control by using tools hosted by the Company like the electrification 
calculators. These tools would allow customers to design an electrification solution that fits their needs.  
 
All of the potential programs will be useful for customers in that they will package relevant 
information in convenient ways. The information will be easily accessible, will have the ability to be 
personalized, and it will comprehensive, comprehendible, and effective. 
 
Finally, the Project Specialist will serve as a dedicated resource to manage the Customer Enablement 
Initiative.  The Project Specialist will need expertise across multiple clean energy technologies in order 
to implement all the associated programs. The Project Specialist will make sure there are fundamental 
consistencies across the programs, too. Allowing multiple, disparate programs to operate in silos 
would limit the impact and success of this critical initiative.  
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
This program places the customer at the center of its focus and supports the Company’s mission to 
enhance the customer experience. Further, this program is in alignment with the Company’s pursuit of 
the CLCPA’s goals. The program addresses enterprise risk by establishing a channel for two-way 
communication between the customer and the Company as the distribution system becomes more 
impacted by the adoption of behind-the-meter technologies.

2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection 
 
An alternative approach for the Company would be to develop stand-alone materials for these 
emerging technologies without incorporating the key lessons and information tools we plan to convey 
through this program. This would ultimately fail because the traditional channels and segmented 
information will not meet customers’ changing needs in order to drive action on emerging technologies 
that support the CLCPA.  
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
 
If the Company abandons this program it would limit the Company’s pursuit of a clean energy future 
and would decrease the likelihood of achieving the CLCPA’s goals. 
 
Risk 2 
 
If the Company abandons this program it risks customers remaining unaware of the impacts these 
electrification technologies have on the grid. This could lead to customers either not adopting these 
technologies or adopting technologies without having sufficient information and tools needed to make 
informed decisions about the most efficient practices.  This would lead to a decreased likelihood of 
achieving the CLCPA’s goals, a decrease in system reliability due to capacity issues, or an increase in 
customers’ costs.  
 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
• 
Helping to achieve the Company and State Clean Energy goals. 
• 
Improved understanding of customer behavior, leading to improved customer satisfaction. 
• 
Enhanced operations leveraging new tools used by the Company.  
• 
Increased engagement and communication with customers and stronger relationships with the 
community. 
• 
Decreased risk of reliability issues.

Project/Program Title: Managed Charging 
Project/Program Manager: Brian Picariello 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 21541705 
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☒ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________

Total Funding Request ($000)

O&M:

☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M:
