Work Description: 
 
To oversee and manage the Hazard Tree Removal Program effectively, proper oversight and span of 
control is necessary.  This request is for one additional in-house FTE to oversee and coordinate the 
efforts of the expanded Hazard Tree Removal Program, as well as other program enhancements and 
initiatives.  
 
Justification Summary: 
 
In order to oversee and support the proposed expanded Hazard Tree Removal Program effectively, an 
O&R Chief Construction Inspector (CCI) position is required.  This position is necessary to provide 
cost effective and productive span of control and oversight.  This would include management of any 
Contractor Supervisors used in customer and municipality outreach, securing of tree removal 
authorization and debris issues, coordination of the work to the crews, post-work review to assure 
proper completion, tracking of production and monitoring of budgets, and other duties. 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy

and direction is not provided. 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
In this rate case filing, the Company is proposing to expand its Hazard Tree Removal Program.  This 
expansion will improve the safety and performance of the Company’s electric system, thereby 
improving the Company’s relationship with its customers.  The addition of an in-house CCI FTE is 
necessary to oversee and coordinate the efforts of the expanded Hazard Tree Removal Program.

coordinate work with the crews, track production and monitor budgets, as well as, communicate with 
local municipalities and stakeholders.

Work Description: 
 
This Vegetation Management Program enhancement is to increase three-phase conductor clearance on 
the Company’s distribution system from 10 feet under, 10 feet to the side, and 15 feet overtop, to 15 
feet under, 15 feet to the side, and 20 feet overtop.  Currently, expanded clearance on three-phase lines 
takes place on Main Line sections and on 34.5kV three-phase lines, but this enhancement is to expand 
this additional clearance to all three-phase conductor throughout the O&R service territory.     
 
Justification Summary: 
 
 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy 
 
 
2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives

N/A

Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1 
Tree contacts continue to impact adversely the safety and reliability of the Company’s electric system, 
both SAIFI and CAIDI, particularly during declared weather events.  Expanding the clearance around 
the distribution system will continue to maintain necessary distances around the conductors during 
weather events, as well as, between cycles and growing seasons. 
 
 
Non-Financial Benefits 
 
Reducing the number of tree contacts, will improve safety and performance to the electric system, 
thereby improving the Company’s relationship with its customers.

1. Cost-benefit analysis (if required) N/A 
 
2. Major financial benefits 
 
3. Total cost 
 
4. Basis for estimate 
 
5. Conclusion 
Yes.  The distribution mainline and three phase conductors make up a significant portion of the O&R 
electric system.  Further enhancing the vegetation clearances will support a reduction in the number of 
tree related contacts and customer interruptions.  This will also support O&R storm hardening 
improvements associated with weather events on the distribution system.

Work Description: 
This request is to increase operating personnel in Electric Operations as follows: 
• 
Electric Trouble Shooter - 11 FTEs;  
• 
Electric Line Technicians – 7 FTEs; and  
• 
Electric Operating Supervisor – 2 FTE.  
 
Justification Summary: 
 
The additional requested personnel are to support system reliability and upgrades, emergency 
response and to right size the organization to meet customer expectations. The overall plan is to 
increase the number of Troubleshooters by an additional 11 FTEs to support a 24/7 operation for 
response to outages and other system emergencies; increase the number of Line Technicians by an 
additional 7 FTEs to support the safe, reliable operation of the distribution system; and increase the 
number of Operating Supervisors by an additional 2 FTEs in order to maintain the appropriate span of 
control.  
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy

4. Definitions 
 
Total Funding Request: All funding requested for program or project over program/project lifecycle or 
for on-going programs the five-year requested amount, including all capital, O&M, retirement. 
 
Cost Savings: Reductions in costs that are currently being incurred (e.g., reduced annual maintenance 
cost relative to today) 
 
Cost Avoidance: Reductions in anticipated future costs that don’t occur today (e.g., anticipated short-
term fixes/maintenance if capital isn’t deployed)

Electric Operations 
2022 
1. Project / Program Summary 
 
Type:  ☐ Project  ☒ Program  
Category:  ☒ Capital  ☐ O&M 
Work Plan Category:  ☐ Regulatory Mandated  ☒ Operationally Required  ☐ Strategic 
Project/Program Title: Electric Operations Vehicle Additions 
Project/Program Manager: Salvatore Muto 
Project/Program Number (Level 1): 
Status:  ☐ Initiation  ☐ Planning  ☐ Execution  ☐ On-going  ☐  ☐ Other: ___________ 
Estimated Start Date: 1/1/2022 
Estimated Date In Service: 1/1/2022 
I. 
Total Funding Request ($000)  
Capital: $ 3,554 
O&M:  
 
J. 
 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Savings ($000) 
☐ 5-Year Gross Cost Avoidance ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
K. 5-Year Ongoing Maintenance Expense ($000) 
O&M: 
Capital: 
L. Investment Payback Period:   
(Years/months) (If applicable) 
Work Description:   
 
This request for 17 truck/vehicles is required to support an increase of 21 FTEs in Electric Operations.  
The capital request includes: 12 overhead bucket trucks, 2 digger derrick vehicles, 2 Supervisor 
vehicles, 1 Chief Construction Inspector (CCI) vehicle and associated capital tools and equipment. 
 
Justification Summary: 
 
These trucks/vehicles are needed to support the additional requested FTEs, in support of system 
reliability, capital upgrades, and emergency response in order to meet customer expectations.   In this 
rate case, the Company is requesting the following additional FTEs: 
 
• 
11 Troubleshooter FTEs to support a 24/7 operation to respond to outages and other system 
emergencies;  
• 
7 Line Technician FTEs to support the safe, reliable operation of the distribution system;  
• 
2 Operating Supervisor FTEs in order to maintain the appropriate span of control; and 
• 
1 Chief Construction Inspector FTE to manage and oversee the Hazard/Danger Tree Program as 
part of the Vegetation Management Program. 
 
Relationship to 5-Year and Long-Range Plans and Enterprise Risk Management Strategy

2. Supplemental Information 
 
Alternatives 
 
Alternative 1 description and reason for rejection:  
By not approving the requested trucks/vehicles, the Company will not be able to support the 
requested FTEs, and thereby will not be able to use them cost-effectively. 
 
Risk of No Action 
 
Risk 1:  
Inability to respond to outages and system emergencies in a timely manner due the lack of 24/7 
coverage in Electric operations. 
 
Non-Financial Benefits
